_ngcontent-sc434="" class="ng-tns-c434-10" _nghost-sc84=""><div _ngcontent-sc84=""><h4 _ngcontent-sc434="" class="card-title"><a _ngcontent-sc434="" class="h-100 ng-tns-c434-10" href="/items/869fb679-78a3-45e9-85ca-8a9cd5f0092f">Health in the Americas 2026: Prioritizing avertable disease burden to maximize impact</a></h4><h6 _ngcontent-sc434="" class="card-date">2026-09-28</h6><ds-truncatable-part _ngcontent-sc434="" _nghost-sc85="" class="ng-tns-c434-10 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-sc85="" class="clamp-default-3 min-3"><div _ngcontent-sc85="" class="content dont-break-out preserve-line-breaks removeFaded"><p _ngcontent-sc434="" class="item-abstract card-text"><span _ngcontent-sc434="" class="ng-tns-c434-10">This edition of Health in the Americas examines how health challenges are evolving across the Region. The Region of the Americas has made important health gains over recent decades. However, regional averages mask inequities across countries and population groups. Furthermore, while coverage of essential health services has expanded, out-of-pocket health spending continues to push many households into financial hardship. The report finds that the burden of disease is shifting decisively toward chronic diseases and disability. A small set of modifiable risk factors, including high blood pressure, high blood sugar, excess bodyweight, unhealthy diets, physical inactivity, and tobacco, alcohol, and drug use, drives multiple high-burden outcomes simultaneously. These patterns signal growing needs for integrated chronic disease management, mental health services, and continuity of care, alongside continued action to prevent premature deaths, violence, and injuries. The report also reveals a critical opportunity for improvement: approximately 40% of the Region’s disease burden in 2023 was estimated to be potentially avertable based on health outcomes already achieved by better-performing countries globally. To seize this opportunity, the report provides practical guidance on translating evidence into action. It shows how countries can set explicit priorities, organize services into priority service bundles, and deliver them through integrated health service delivery networks. Success requires strong stewardship and governance. Through the evidence and tools in this report, countries can identify where health gains are greatest and design interventions that deliver measurable health improvements.  </span></p></div><button _ngcontent-sc85="" dsdragclick="" class="btn btn-link p-0 expandButton"><i _ngcontent-sc85="" class="fas fa-angle-down"></i><span _ngcontent-sc85="" class="ml-1">Show _ngcontent-sc434="" class="ng-tns-c434-12" _nghost-sc84=""><div _ngcontent-sc84=""><h4 _ngcontent-sc434="" class="card-title"><a _ngcontent-sc434="" class="h-100 ng-tns-c434-12" href="/items/58457d27-8906-410f-b94c-3a2f04bf1bd7">Caribbean Action Plan on Health and Climate Change 2026–2031</a></h4><h6 _ngcontent-sc434="" class="card-date">2026-09-25</h6><ds-truncatable-part _ngcontent-sc434="" _nghost-sc85="" class="ng-tns-c434-12 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-sc85="" class="clamp-default-3 min-3"><div _ngcontent-sc85="" class="content dont-break-out preserve-line-breaks removeFaded"><p _ngcontent-sc434="" class="item-abstract card-text"><span _ngcontent-sc434="" class="ng-tns-c434-12">Climate change is reshaping health risks across the Caribbean. Increasingly frequent and intense hurricanes, heatwaves, sea-level rise, and changing patterns of climate-sensitive diseases threaten lives and livelihoods, disrupt essential services and place growing pressure on health systems. These impacts are especially significant for Small Island Developing States, where geographical, environmental, social, and economic vulnerabilities can magnify health consequences and undermine hard-won development gains. 

Important progress has been made across the Caribbean since the first regional Action Plan was adopted in 2019. Countries have strengthened leadership on climate change and health, advanced Health National Adaptation Plans, expanded climate-informed surveillance and early warning capacities, improved the safety and environmental sustainability of health facilities, and invested in workforce development. These achievements demonstrate what coordinated regional action can accomplish. However, despite the achievements, persistent gaps in financing, institutional capacity, community engagement, and access to strategic information necessitate continued action. 

The Caribbean Action Plan on Health and Climate Change 2026–2031 provides a shared framework for the next phase. It supports country-led action through four strategic lines: Governance and Leadership; Climate-Resilient Health Systems (Adaptation); Low-Carbon Health Systems (Mitigation); and Resources and Climate Finance. Refined indicators strengthen accountability and help countries monitor results while retaining the flexibility to reflect national contexts and priorities. 

Successful implementation will depend on sustained political commitment, multisectoral collaboration, and strong regional partnerships. The Caribbean Community (CARICOM), the Caribbean Community Climate Change Centre (CCCCC), the Caribbean Institute for Meteorology and Hydrology (CIMH), the Caribbean Public Health Agency (CARPHA), and The University of the West Indies (UWI) bring complementary political, technical, and academic expertise. Civil society, communities, youth, governmental and nongovernmental partners, the media and other stakeholders are also vital to inclusive and effective action. 

By connecting evidence, policy, practice, and investment, the Action Plan offers Caribbean countries and territories a practical pathway to protect health, maintain essential services, and build more resilient, equitable, and environmentally sustainable health systems. It is both a regional commitment and an invitation to act – so that every community is better prepared for the health challenges of a changing climate.   </span></p></div><button _ngcontent-sc85="" dsdragclick="" class="btn btn-link p-0 expandButton"><i _ngcontent-sc85="" class="fas fa-angle-down"></i><span _ngcontent-sc85="" class="ml-1">Show href="/items/09377e5f-f6ae-4a73-a85e-92697bd0c065">Boletim de Imunização; v.48, n.1, agosto de 2026</a></h4><h6 _ngcontent-sc434="" class="card-date">2026-09-24</h6><ds-truncatable-part _ngcontent-sc434="" _nghost-sc85="" class="ng-tns-c434-14 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-sc85="" class="clamp-default-3 min-3"><div _ngcontent-sc85="" class="content dont-break-out preserve-line-breaks removeFaded"><p _ngcontent-sc434="" class="item-abstract card-text"><span _ngcontent-sc434="" class="ng-tns-c434-14">A edição de agosto de 2026 do Boletim de Imunização reúne informações sobre alguns dos principais avanços, experiências e desafios atuais em matéria de vacinação na Região das Américas. Entre os destaques estão os resultados das estimativas da OMS e do UNICEF sobre a cobertura nacional de imunização para 2025 (WUENIC 2025), que mostram avanços na vacinação infantil, embora ainda existam lacunas significativas, especialmente na cobertura contra o sarampo. Esta edição também apresenta os resultados de um estudo realizado pela OPAS e pelo Ministério da Saúde da Argentina sobre os fatores que influenciam a vacinação de mulheres grávidas contra a gripe, a COVID-19 e o vírus sincicial respiratório (VSR), bem como uma iniciativa realizada com as comunidades indígenas Inga de Putumayo, na Colômbia, para fortalecer a aceitação e a confiança na vacinação contra o papilomavírus humano (HPV). Além disso, destaca a experiência do Centro de Desenvolvimento de Competências do Programa Ampliado de Imunização no Estado Plurinacional da Bolívia, que visa fortalecer as capacidades dos profissionais de saúde, e apresenta os avanços do Chile em saúde digital por meio do módulo “Tus Vacunas e Inmunizaciones”. A publicação é complementada com novos recursos para apoiar os programas de imunização, incluindo diretrizes para o microplanejamento de atividades de vacinação, um curso on-line sobre confiança nas vacinas e desinformação, e um painel com dados sobre a cobertura da vacinação contra a gripe nas Américas.</span></p></div><button _ngcontent-sc85="" dsdragclick="" class="btn btn-link p-0 expandButton"><i _ngcontent-sc85="" class="fas fa-angle-down"></i><span _ngcontent-sc85="" class="ml-1">Show _ngcontent-sc434="" class="ng-tns-c434-16" _nghost-sc84=""><div _ngcontent-sc84=""><h4 _ngcontent-sc434="" class="card-title"><a _ngcontent-sc434="" class="h-100 ng-tns-c434-16" href="/items/18d456b8-4093-42c3-9103-97451953deeb">Bulletin d'immunisation, v.48, n.1, août 2026</a></h4><h6 _ngcontent-sc434="" class="card-date">2026-09-24</h6><ds-truncatable-part _ngcontent-sc434="" _nghost-sc85="" class="ng-tns-c434-16 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-sc85="" class="clamp-default-3 min-3"><div _ngcontent-sc85="" class="content dont-break-out preserve-line-breaks removeFaded"><p _ngcontent-sc434="" class="item-abstract card-text"><span _ngcontent-sc434="" class="ng-tns-c434-16">Le numéro d’août 2026 du Bulletin de la vaccination rassemble des informations sur certains des principaux développements, expériences et défis actuels en matière de vaccination dans la Région des Amériques. Parmi les points forts, on peut citer les résultats des estimations de l’OMS et de l’UNICEF concernant la couverture vaccinale nationale pour 2025 (WUENIC 2025), qui montrent des progrès en matière de vaccination infantile, même si d’importantes lacunes subsistent, notamment en ce qui concerne la couverture vaccinale contre la rougeole. Ce numéro présente également les conclusions d’une étude menée par l’OPS et le ministère argentin de la Santé sur les facteurs influençant la vaccination des femmes enceintes contre la grippe, la COVID-19 et le virus respiratoire syncytial (VRS), ainsi qu’une initiative menée auprès des communautés autochtones Inga du Putumayo, en Colombie, visant à renforcer l’acceptation et la confiance dans la vaccination contre le papillomavirus humain (HPV). Il met également en avant l’expérience du Centre de développement des compétences du Programme élargi de vaccination (PEV) de l’État plurinational de Bolivie, qui vise à renforcer les capacités du personnel de santé, et présente les progrès réalisés par le Chili en matière de santé numérique grâce au module « Tus Vacunas e Inmunizaciones ». La publication est complétée par de nouvelles ressources destinées à soutenir les programmes de vaccination, notamment des lignes directrices pour la micro-planification des activités de vaccination, un cours en ligne sur la confiance dans les vaccins et la désinformation, ainsi qu’un tableau de bord présentant des données sur la couverture vaccinale contre la grippe dans les Amériques.</span></p></div><button _ngcontent-sc85="" dsdragclick="" class="btn btn-link p-0 expandButton"><i _ngcontent-sc85="" class="fas fa-angle-down"></i><span _ngcontent-sc85="" _ngcontent-sc434="" class="ng-tns-c434-18" _nghost-sc84=""><div _ngcontent-sc84=""><h4 _ngcontent-sc434="" class="card-title"><a _ngcontent-sc434="" class="h-100 ng-tns-c434-18" href="/items/989f2411-2757-4d41-9352-e1b9d8d5a4dd">Diseño de etiquetas frontales de advertencia eficaces para proteger a la salud pública frente a los productos alimentarios poco saludables. Evidencia, principios de política y especificaciones de diseño para respaldar la adopción e implementación de un sistema eficaz de etiquetado frontal de advertencia en la Región de las Américas</a></h4><h6 _ngcontent-sc434="" class="card-date">2026-09-24</h6><ds-truncatable-part _ngcontent-sc434="" _nghost-sc85="" class="ng-tns-c434-18 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-sc85="" class="clamp-default-3 min-3"><div _ngcontent-sc85="" class="content dont-break-out preserve-line-breaks removeFaded"><p _ngcontent-sc434="" class="item-abstract card-text"><span _ngcontent-sc434="" class="ng-tns-c434-18">El etiquetado frontal de advertencia (EFA) ha surgido como una estrategia regulatoria clave para abordar la carga creciente de enfermedades no transmisibles (ENT) en la Región de las Américas. Varios gobiernos a lo largo de la Región están adoptando sistemas de EFA para brindar información clara, simple y eficaz que permite a los consumidores identificar rápidamente productos con cantidades excesivas de azúcares, grasas, sodio y otros componentes asociados con resultados adversos para la salud. 

Esta nota técnica presenta un marco que integra la salud pública, la nutrición, y el diseño de la información, consolidando la mejor evidencia disponible y las mejores prácticas a nivel internacional para apoyar a los tomadores de decisiones, investigadores, y funcionarios de los ministerios de salud en el desarrollo, adopción e implementación de sistemas de EFA efectivos. La nota técnica describe tres tipos de advertencias y cada una corresponde a un riesgo nutricional o de la composición del producto dentro de una escala de fortaleza de la evidencia epidemiológica y prioridades de salud pública: la advertencia triangular de productos ultraprocesados, la advertencia octagonal de EXCESO y las advertencias precautorias. Las especificaciones de diseño varían de acuerdo con el propósito que cada etiqueta busca alcanzar.  

La nota técnica también provee recomendaciones sobre elementos gráficos y de diseño - incluyendo forma, tamaño, color, contraste, tipografía y colocación de las etiquetas de advertencia. Estos elementos en conjunto tienen la intención de asegurar que las etiquetas atraen la atención, se comprenden fácilmente y mejoran la habilidad de los consumidores para tomar decisiones alimentarias más saludables. De la misma manera, para apoyar el diseño de sistemas de EFA efectivos, la OPS establece y provee principios de legibilidad y visibilidad para la declaración de riesgos, prominencia, integridad gráfica, legibilidad de la tipografía, aislamiento de la información, brevedad representación multimodal y posición estratégica.  

Cuando se desarrolla con integridad estructural y se apoyan en la evaluación continua, el EFA puede transformar los entornos alimentarios y utilizarse como un mecanismo efectivo para reducir la mortalidad prematura y resguardar la salud pública en la Región de las Américas.</span></p></div><button _ngcontent-sc85="" dsdragclick="" class="btn btn-link p-0 expandButton"><i _ngcontent-sc85="" class="fas fa-angle-down"></i><span _ngcontent-sc85="" class="ml-1">Show _ngcontent-sc434="" class="ng-tns-c434-20" _nghost-sc84=""><div _ngcontent-sc84=""><h4 _ngcontent-sc434="" class="card-title"><a _ngcontent-sc434="" class="h-100 ng-tns-c434-20" href="/items/c49f751b-6eac-432c-896c-9fff3a80dc63">Immunization Newsletter, v.48, n.2, August 2026</a></h4><h6 _ngcontent-sc434="" class="card-date">2026-09-24</h6><ds-truncatable-part _ngcontent-sc434="" _nghost-sc85="" class="ng-tns-c434-20 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-sc85="" class="clamp-default-3 min-3"><div _ngcontent-sc85="" class="content dont-break-out preserve-line-breaks removeFaded"><p _ngcontent-sc434="" class="item-abstract card-text"><span _ngcontent-sc434="" class="ng-tns-c434-20">The August 2026 issue of the Immunization Bulletin compiles information on some of the key developments, experiences, and current challenges in vaccination in the Region of the Americas.

Highlights include the results of WHO and UNICEF estimates of national immunization coverage for 2025 (WUENIC 2025), which show progress in childhood vaccination, although significant gaps remain, particularly in measles coverage.

This issue also presents the findings of a study conducted by PAHO and the Argentine Ministry of Health on the factors influencing the vaccination of pregnant women against influenza, COVID-19, and respiratory syncytial virus (RSV), as well as an initiative carried out with the Inga indigenous communities of Putumayo, Colombia, to strengthen acceptance of and confidence in vaccination against human papillomavirus (HPV).

It also highlights the experience of the Expanded Program on Immunization’s Competency Development Center in the Plurinational State of Bolivia, aimed at strengthening the capacities of health personnel, and presents Chile’s progress in digital health through the “Tus Vacunas e Inmunizaciones” module. The publication is rounded out with new resources to support immunization programs, including guidelines for micro-planning vaccination activities, an online course on vaccine confidence and misinformation, and a dashboard with data on influenza vaccination coverage in the Americas.</span></p></div><button _ngcontent-sc85="" dsdragclick="" class="btn btn-link p-0 expandButton"><i _ngcontent-sc85="" class="fas fa-angle-down"></i><span _ngcontent-sc85="" class="ml-1">Show _ngcontent-sc434="" class="ng-tns-c434-22" _nghost-sc84=""><div _ngcontent-sc84=""><h4 _ngcontent-sc434="" class="card-title"><a _ngcontent-sc434="" class="h-100 ng-tns-c434-22" href="/items/4569ef8e-14c6-46e0-b01b-3c9a62a88766">HEARTS en las Américas: de la evidencia a una cultura de calidad en la atención primaria</a></h4><h6 _ngcontent-sc434="" class="card-date">2026-09-24</h6><ds-truncatable-part _ngcontent-sc434="" _nghost-sc85="" class="ng-tns-c434-22 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-sc85="" class="clamp-default-3 min-3"><div _ngcontent-sc85="" class="content dont-break-out preserve-line-breaks removeFaded"><p _ngcontent-sc434="" class="item-abstract card-text"><span _ngcontent-sc434="" class="ng-tns-c434-22">Las enfermedades cardiovasculares causan millones de muertes cada año en la Región de las Américas, y la hipertensión arterial no controlada continúa siendo su principal factor de riesgo modificable. Pese a los avances, el control de la presión arterial entre las personas con hipertensión sigue siendo insuficiente en la Región. Esta no es principalmente una brecha de conocimiento, sino de implementación. Refleja sistemas de salud de desempeño mediano o bajo que aún no logran traducir la evidencia científica y los marcos conceptuales disponibles en una atención efectiva, equitativa, continua y con alcance poblacional. La insuficiente financiación de la atención primaria, la fragmentación de los servicios, la variabilidad clínica y las limitaciones en el acceso a medicamentos, tecnologías, trabajo en equipo e información siguen reduciendo el impacto de intervenciones eficaces.

HEARTS ofrece una respuesta concreta a esta brecha. Concebido originalmente por la Organización Mundial de la Salud como un paquete técnico, en las Américas ha evolucionado hacia una estrategia de salud pública liderada por los países para transformar los servicios de atención primaria mediante indicadores medibles y la mejora continua de la calidad. Con 34 países comprometidos y más de 14 000 establecimientos participantes, HEARTS utiliza la hipertensión como condición trazadora y punto de entrada para articular protocolos estandarizados, medicamentos esenciales, dispositivos validados para medir la presión arterial, atención basada en equipos multidisciplinarios, manejo basado en el riesgo y sistemas de monitoreo. HEARTS 2.0 amplía esta plataforma hacia la prevención y el manejo integrado del riesgo cardiovascular, renal y metabólico en la atención primaria.</span></p></div><button _ngcontent-sc85="" dsdragclick="" class="btn btn-link p-0 expandButton"><i _ngcontent-sc85="" class="fas fa-angle-down"></i><span _ngcontent-sc85="" class="ml-1">Show more</span></button><!----></div></ds-truncatable-part><!----></div></ds-truncatable></div></div></ds-item-search-result-grid-element-paho><!----></ds-listable-object-component-loader></div><div _ngcontent-sc434="" class="ng-tns-c434-24" _nghost-sc84=""><div _ngcontent-sc84=""><h4 _ngcontent-sc434="" class="card-title"><a _ngcontent-sc434="" class="h-100 ng-tns-c434-24" href="/items/0d62184f-3449-4a75-b9f9-2d1a12769645">PAHO Case: Fostering partnerships for a healthier future in the Americas. Brochure</a></h4><h6 _ngcontent-sc434="" class="card-date">2026-09-24</h6><ds-truncatable-part _ngcontent-sc434="" _nghost-sc85="" class="ng-tns-c434-24 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-sc85="" class="clamp-default-3 min-3"><div _ngcontent-sc85="" class="content dont-break-out preserve-line-breaks removeFaded"><p _ngcontent-sc434="" class="item-abstract card-text"><span _ngcontent-sc434="" class="ng-tns-c434-24">The Pan American Health Organization (PAHO) is committed to advancing public health and improving well-being across the Region of the Americas through technical cooperation, strategic partnerships, and resource mobilization. To clearly articulate the Organization’s value proposition and support more strategic and effective investment in health, PAHO developed the PAHO Case, a comprehensive publication that demonstrates the importance of investing in health and strengthening health systems throughout the Region.

The PAHO Case presents compelling evidence on the health, social, and economic benefits of investing in proven interventions and collective regional action. It highlights the expected return on investment for Member States, international donors, development banks, foundations, the private sector, and other partners, while underscoring the broader contribution of these investments to achieving national, regional, and global health and sustainable development goals.

The publication outlines PAHO’s forward-looking vision for accelerating progress toward universal health, expanding equitable access to quality primary health care, advancing the digital transformation of health systems, strengthening preparedness for emergencies and health threats, and promoting sustainable solutions that build long-term resilience. It also showcases examples of PAHO’s most impactful and innovative initiatives, demonstrating how regional cooperation and targeted investments can generate measurable results and improve health outcomes.

As both a strategic communications and advocacy tool, the PAHO Case illustrates PAHO’s unique role as the leading public health organization in the Americas and a trusted partner for countries and stakeholders across the Region. The publication highlights PAHO’s achievements, current contributions, remaining challenges, and future opportunities, emphasizing that investing in PAHO means investing in healthier populations, stronger health systems, and more resilient societies throughout the Americas.</span></p></div><button _ngcontent-sc85="" dsdragclick="" class="btn btn-link p-0 expandButton"><i _ngcontent-sc85="" class="fas fa-angle-down"></i><span _ngcontent-sc85="" class="ml-1">Show edition of Health in the Americas examines how health challenges are evolving across the Region. The Region of the Americas has made important health gains over recent decades. However, regional averages mask inequities across countries and population groups. Furthermore, while coverage of essential health services has expanded, out-of-pocket health spending continues to push many households into financial hardship. The report finds that the burden of disease is shifting decisively toward chronic diseases and disability. A small set of modifiable risk factors, including high blood pressure, high blood sugar, excess bodyweight, unhealthy diets, physical inactivity, and tobacco, alcohol, and drug use, drives multiple high-burden outcomes simultaneously. These patterns signal growing needs for integrated chronic disease management, mental health services, and continuity of care, alongside continued action to prevent premature deaths, violence, and injuries. The report also reveals a critical opportunity for improvement: approximately 40% of the Region’s disease burden in 2023 was estimated to be potentially avertable based on health outcomes already achieved by better-performing countries globally. To seize this opportunity, the report provides practical guidance on translating evidence into action. It shows how countries can set explicit priorities, organize services into priority service bundles, and deliver them through integrated health service delivery networks. Success requires strong stewardship and governance. Through the evidence and tools in this report, countries can identify where health gains are greatest and design interventions that deliver measurable health improvements.  &q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:0}],&q;dc.identifier.citation&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;28d01a92-b02d-4e15-8a9f-191b1174de6c&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;Pan American Health Organization. Health in the Americas 2026: Prioritizing\navertable disease burden to maximize impact. Washington, D.C.: PAHO; 2026. American Health Organization&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.date.accessioned&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;11f2edcf-8d3c-4415-9153-400863ddacb3&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;2026-09-18T19:12:07Z&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.date.available&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;87ed9258-06e1-4550-8f59-566d9efd9021&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;2026-09-18T19:12:07Z&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.date.issued&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;88180a81-d3bc-485a-8b8a-88ae88dd1d52&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;2026-09-25&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:0}],&q;dc.description.abstract&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;93b8176b-345f-4f8c-b0c6-179d9c72edcf&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;Climate change is reshaping health risks across the Caribbean. Increasingly frequent and intense hurricanes, heatwaves, sea-level rise, and changing patterns of climate-sensitive diseases threaten lives and livelihoods, disrupt essential services and place growing pressure on health systems. These impacts are especially significant for Small Island Developing States, where geographical, environmental, social, and economic vulnerabilities can magnify health consequences and undermine hard-won development gains. \n\nImportant progress has been made across the Caribbean since the first regional Action Plan was adopted in 2019. Countries have strengthened leadership on climate change and health, advanced Health National Adaptation Plans, expanded climate-informed surveillance and early warning capacities, improved the safety and environmental sustainability of health facilities, and invested in workforce development. These achievements demonstrate what coordinated regional action can accomplish. However, despite the achievements, persistent gaps in financing, institutional capacity, community engagement, and access to strategic information necessitate continued action. \n\nThe Caribbean Action Plan on Health and Climate Change 2026–2031 provides a shared framework for the next phase. It supports country-led action through four strategic lines: Governance and Leadership; Climate-Resilient Health Systems (Adaptation); Low-Carbon Health Systems (Mitigation); and Resources and Climate Finance. Refined indicators strengthen accountability and help countries monitor results while retaining the flexibility to reflect national contexts and priorities. \n\nSuccessful implementation will depend on sustained political commitment, multisectoral collaboration, and strong regional partnerships. The Caribbean Community (CARICOM), the Caribbean Community Climate Change Centre (CCCCC), the Caribbean Institute for Meteorology and Hydrology (CIMH), the Caribbean Public Health Agency (CARPHA), and The University of the West Indies (UWI) bring complementary political, technical, and academic expertise. Civil society, communities, youth, governmental and nongovernmental partners, the media and other stakeholders are also vital to inclusive and effective action. \n\nBy connecting evidence, policy, practice, and investment, the Action Plan offers Caribbean countries and territories a practical pathway to protect health, maintain essential services, and build more resilient, equitable, and environmentally sustainable health systems. It is both a regional commitment and an invitation to act – so that every community is better prepared for the health challenges of a changing climate. Saúde&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.date.accessioned&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;a1937a1c-ba0c-4bae-b88d-b5cce2d4f37b&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;2026-09-24T12:09:21Z&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.date.available&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;fc171aa3-2c66-4719-9c49-ae49dccf5d9d&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;2026-09-24T12:09:21Z&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.date.issued&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;17c42e4e-1926-436e-ad04-0b3758cd7f5a&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;2026-09-24&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.description.abstract&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;dd88e869-a885-4b1e-8582-642d8635472e&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;A edição de agosto de 2026 do Boletim de Imunização reúne informações sobre alguns dos principais avanços, experiências e desafios atuais em matéria de vacinação na Região das Américas. Entre os destaques estão os resultados das estimativas da OMS e do UNICEF sobre a cobertura nacional de imunização para 2025 (WUENIC 2025), que mostram avanços na vacinação infantil, embora ainda existam lacunas significativas, especialmente na cobertura contra o sarampo. Esta edição também apresenta os resultados de um estudo realizado pela OPAS e pelo Ministério da Saúde da Argentina sobre os fatores que influenciam a vacinação de mulheres grávidas contra a gripe, a COVID-19 e o vírus sincicial respiratório (VSR), bem como uma iniciativa realizada com as comunidades indígenas Inga de Putumayo, na Colômbia, para fortalecer a aceitação e a confiança na vacinação contra o papilomavírus humano (HPV). Além disso, destaca a experiência do Centro de Desenvolvimento de Competências do Programa Ampliado de Imunização no Estado Plurinacional da Bolívia, que visa fortalecer as capacidades dos profissionais de saúde, e apresenta os avanços do Chile em saúde digital por meio do módulo “Tus Vacunas e Inmunizaciones”. A publicação é complementada com novos recursos para apoiar os programas de imunização, incluindo diretrizes para o microplanejamento de atividades de vacinação, um curso on-line sobre confiança nas vacinas e desinformação, e um painel com dados sobre a cobertura da vacinação contra a gripe nas Américas.&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.identifier.govdoc&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;160d0128-93c9-41d9-bff5-a438aa4790cb&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;OPAS/CIM/26-0010&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.identifier.uri&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;75793dd4-e692-4331-9ca0-b7106154e528&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;https://iris.paho.org/handle/10665.2/72316&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.language.iso&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;7f9b7c1a-13d1-43a8-9904-d3ce18ad77aa&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;pt&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.publisher&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;a7f932a2-4bd4-402e-86aa-b6c74ead2ddf&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;OPAS&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.relation.ispartofjournal&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;02e38465-28ad-4827-87a1-04c33a9b7b55&q;,&q;language&q;:&q;EN&q;,&q;value&q;:&q;PAHO numéro d’août 2026 du Bulletin de la vaccination rassemble des informations sur certains des principaux développements, expériences et défis actuels en matière de vaccination dans la Région des Amériques. Parmi les points forts, on peut citer les résultats des estimations de l’OMS et de l’UNICEF concernant la couverture vaccinale nationale pour 2025 (WUENIC 2025), qui montrent des progrès en matière de vaccination infantile, même si d’importantes lacunes subsistent, notamment en ce qui concerne la couverture vaccinale contre la rougeole. Ce numéro présente également les conclusions d’une étude menée par l’OPS et le ministère argentin de la Santé sur les facteurs influençant la vaccination des femmes enceintes contre la grippe, la COVID-19 et le virus respiratoire syncytial (VRS), ainsi qu’une initiative menée auprès des communautés autochtones Inga du Putumayo, en Colombie, visant à renforcer l’acceptation et la confiance dans la vaccination contre le papillomavirus humain (HPV). Il met également en avant l’expérience du Centre de développement des compétences du Programme élargi de vaccination (PEV) de l’État plurinational de Bolivie, qui vise à renforcer les capacités du personnel de santé, et présente les progrès réalisés par le Chili en matière de santé numérique grâce au module « Tus Vacunas e Inmunizaciones ». La publication est complétée par de nouvelles ressources destinées à soutenir les programmes de vaccination, notamment des lignes directrices pour la micro-planification des activités de vaccination, un cours en ligne sur la confiance dans les vaccins et la désinformation, ainsi qu’un tableau de bord présentant des données sur la couverture vaccinale contre la grippe dans les -9352-e1b9d8d5a4dd/thumbnail&q;},&q;relateditemlistconfigs&q;:{&q;href&q;:&q;https://iris.paho.org/server/api/core/items/989f2411-2757-4d41-9352-e1b9d8d5a4dd/relateditemlistconfigs&q;},&q;self&q;:{&q;href&q;:&q;https://iris.paho.org/server/api/core/items/989f2411-2757-4d41-9352-e1b9d8d5a4dd&q;}},&q;_name&q;:&q;Diseño de etiquetas frontales de advertencia eficaces para proteger a la salud pública frente a los productos alimentarios poco saludables. Evidencia, principios de política y especificaciones de diseño para respaldar la adopción e implementación de un sistema eficaz de etiquetado frontal de advertencia en la Región de las Américas&q;,&q;id&q;:&q;989f2411-2757-4d41-9352-e1b9d8d5a4dd&q;,&q;uuid&q;:&q;989f2411-2757-4d41-9352-e1b9d8d5a4dd&q;,&q;type&q;:&q;item&q;,&q;metadata&q;:{&q;dc.contributor.corporatename&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;2c2c3c31-0e32-4281-98d1-59cfaa99be65&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;Organización Panamericana de la Salud&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.date.accessioned&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;ba1de423-6b2d-45fc-9768-833b0534e1e2&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;2026-09-24T12:35:10Z&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.date.available&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;6d030232-48e7-46e0-aac7-faa9bc6e333a&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;2026-09-24T12:35:10Z&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.date.issued&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;bf8af814-4d26-440f-9609-3872da4fdfd3&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;2026-09-24&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.description.abstract&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;91dd00a0-1bfa-4406-9c2b-b8fa502ad4c4&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;El etiquetado frontal de advertencia (EFA) ha surgido como una estrategia regulatoria clave para abordar la carga creciente de enfermedades no transmisibles (ENT) en la Región de las Américas. Varios gobiernos a lo largo de la Región están adoptando sistemas de EFA para brindar información clara, simple y eficaz que permite a los consumidores identificar rápidamente productos con cantidades excesivas de azúcares, grasas, sodio y otros componentes asociados con resultados adversos para la salud. \n\nEsta nota técnica presenta un marco que integra la salud pública, la nutrición, y el diseño de la información, consolidando la mejor evidencia disponible y las mejores prácticas a nivel internacional para apoyar a los tomadores de decisiones, investigadores, y funcionarios de los ministerios de salud en el desarrollo, adopción e implementación de sistemas de EFA efectivos. La nota técnica describe tres tipos de advertencias y cada una corresponde a un riesgo nutricional o de la composición del producto dentro de una escala de fortaleza de la evidencia epidemiológica y prioridades de salud pública: la advertencia triangular de productos ultraprocesados, la advertencia octagonal de EXCESO y las advertencias precautorias. Las especificaciones de diseño varían de acuerdo con el propósito que cada etiqueta busca alcanzar.  \n\nLa nota técnica también provee recomendaciones sobre elementos gráficos y de diseño - incluyendo forma, tamaño, color, contraste, tipografía y colocación de las etiquetas de advertencia. Estos elementos en conjunto tienen la intención de asegurar que las etiquetas atraen la atención, se comprenden fácilmente y mejoran la habilidad de los consumidores para tomar decisiones alimentarias más saludables. De la misma manera, para apoyar el diseño de sistemas de EFA efectivos, la OPS establece y provee principios de legibilidad y visibilidad para la declaración de riesgos, prominencia, integridad gráfica, legibilidad de la tipografía, aislamiento de la información, brevedad representación multimodal y posición estratégica.  \n\nCuando se desarrolla con integridad estructural y se apoyan en la evaluación continua, el EFA puede transformar los entornos alimentarios y utilizarse como un mecanismo efectivo para reducir la mortalidad prematura y resguardar la salud pública en la Región de las Américas.&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.identifier.govdoc&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;ebc3f6c8-62e7-49bb-9680-e0e793cd429c&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;OPS/NMH/RF/26-0007&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.identifier.uri&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;cf3f3edd-98db-4857-8cdb-3c1d71059ae1&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;https://iris.paho.org/handle/10665.2/72317&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.language.iso&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;72b303b5-5b8a-444a-86b6-edf4bdd8e581&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;es&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.publisher&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;32c70154-2b7e-4e41-bf9e-c13d0494b89f&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;OPS&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.rights&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;3c59c83b-b4b3-4d9b-b086-5883b085e434&q;,&q;language&q;:&q;en&q;,&q;value&q;:&q;Attribution-NonCommercial-ShareAlike August 2026 issue of the Immunization Bulletin compiles information on some of the key developments, experiences, and current challenges in vaccination in the Region of the Americas.\n\nHighlights include the results of WHO and UNICEF estimates of national immunization coverage for 2025 (WUENIC 2025), which show progress in childhood vaccination, although significant gaps remain, particularly in measles coverage.\n\nThis issue also presents the findings of a study conducted by PAHO and the Argentine Ministry of Health on the factors influencing the vaccination of pregnant women against influenza, COVID-19, and respiratory syncytial virus (RSV), as well as an initiative carried out with the Inga indigenous communities of Putumayo, Colombia, to strengthen acceptance of and confidence in vaccination against human papillomavirus (HPV).\n\nIt also highlights the experience of the Expanded Program on Immunization’s Competency Development Center in the Plurinational State of Bolivia, aimed at strengthening the capacities of health personnel, and presents Chile’s progress in digital health through the “Tus Vacunas e Inmunizaciones” module. The publication is rounded out with new resources to support immunization programs, including guidelines for micro-planning vaccination activities, an online course on vaccine confidence and misinformation, and a dashboard with data on influenza vaccination coverage in the Americas.&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.identifier.govdoc&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;94eba0c5-d117-4a18-8d4a-f47f32cbf525&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;PAHO/CIM/26-0010&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.identifier.uri&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;f5d0381a-6b8a-4ae5-86e0-c7418383f1f3&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;https://iris.paho.org/handle/10665.2/72314&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.language.iso&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;8402227d-72c4-4c6a-b40d-c498767f12af&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;en&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.publisher&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;4eb97b63-dc67-4fc4-a7ea-9ac8a965199e&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;PAHO&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.relation.ispartofjournal&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;aa9d11bc-24c3-423b-b214-9c5492698ab1&q;,&q;language&q;:&q;EN&q;,&q;value&q;:&q;PAHO Ordúñez&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.date.accessioned&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;d31764e5-6fd1-4c14-a5a1-bb5fdf8e00d4&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;2026-09-24T11:22:50Z&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.date.available&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;d0295abd-130f-4dd1-aeed-08cd95ac975c&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;2026-09-24T11:22:50Z&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.date.issued&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;7dbdf714-99d6-4f28-aa1c-991e457e8721&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;2026-09-24&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.description.abstract&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;53b53745-212a-4c82-8026-3b37f01d65f4&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;Las enfermedades cardiovasculares causan millones de muertes cada año en la Región de las Américas, y la hipertensión arterial no controlada continúa siendo su principal factor de riesgo modificable. Pese a los avances, el control de la presión arterial entre las personas con hipertensión sigue siendo insuficiente en la Región. Esta no es principalmente una brecha de conocimiento, sino de implementación. Refleja sistemas de salud de desempeño mediano o bajo que aún no logran traducir la evidencia científica y los marcos conceptuales disponibles en una atención efectiva, equitativa, continua y con alcance poblacional. La insuficiente financiación de la atención primaria, la fragmentación de los servicios, la variabilidad clínica y las limitaciones en el acceso a medicamentos, tecnologías, trabajo en equipo e información siguen reduciendo el impacto de intervenciones eficaces.\n\nHEARTS ofrece una respuesta concreta a esta brecha. Concebido originalmente por la Organización Mundial de la Salud como un paquete técnico, en las Américas ha evolucionado hacia una estrategia de salud pública liderada por los países para transformar los servicios de atención primaria mediante indicadores medibles y la mejora continua de la calidad. Con 34 países comprometidos y más de 14 000 establecimientos participantes, HEARTS utiliza la hipertensión como condición trazadora y punto de entrada para articular protocolos estandarizados, medicamentos esenciales, dispositivos validados para medir la presión arterial, atención basada en equipos multidisciplinarios, manejo basado en el riesgo y sistemas de monitoreo. HEARTS 2.0 amplía esta plataforma hacia la prevención y el manejo integrado del riesgo cardiovascular, renal y metabólico en la atención primaria.&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.identifier.citation&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;89244806-deab-4989-a91c-980a15236f19&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;Londoño Agudelo E, Ordunez P. HEARTS en las Américas: de la evidencia a una cultura de calidad en la atención primaria. Case: Fostering partnerships for a healthier future in the Americas. Brochure&q;,&q;id&q;:&q;0d62184f-3449-4a75-b9f9-2d1a12769645&q;,&q;uuid&q;:&q;0d62184f-3449-4a75-b9f9-2d1a12769645&q;,&q;type&q;:&q;item&q;,&q;metadata&q;:{&q;dc.contributor.corporatename&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;8b56d4fd-ce3c-44cc-a7c2-af7c9041d9ca&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;Pan American Health Organization&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.date.accessioned&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;68a27735-c73a-41a4-9939-061560b257e0&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;2026-09-24T12:48:11Z&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.date.available&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;02769a46-9ff7-4518-bd0a-33863a424a8a&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;2026-09-24T12:48:11Z&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.date.issued&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;b3011e82-1183-4f6a-974c-b940d776516e&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;2026-09-24&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.description.abstract&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;a664ade5-f0ec-4bde-a774-e816889f0da5&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;The Pan American Health Organization (PAHO) is committed to advancing public health and improving well-being across the Region of the Americas through technical cooperation, strategic partnerships, and resource mobilization. To clearly articulate the Organization’s value proposition and support more strategic and effective investment in health, PAHO developed the PAHO Case, a comprehensive publication that demonstrates the importance of investing in health and strengthening health systems throughout the Region.\n\nThe PAHO Case presents compelling evidence on the health, social, and economic benefits of investing in proven interventions and collective regional action. It highlights the expected return on investment for Member States, international donors, development banks, foundations, the private sector, and other partners, while underscoring the broader contribution of these investments to achieving national, regional, and global health and sustainable development goals.\n\nThe publication outlines PAHO’s forward-looking vision for accelerating progress toward universal health, expanding equitable access to quality primary health care, advancing the digital transformation of health systems, strengthening preparedness for emergencies and health threats, and promoting sustainable solutions that build long-term resilience. It also showcases examples of PAHO’s most impactful and innovative initiatives, demonstrating how regional cooperation and targeted investments can generate measurable results and improve health outcomes.\n\nAs both a strategic communications and advocacy tool, the PAHO Case illustrates PAHO’s unique role as the leading public health organization in the Americas and a trusted partner for countries and stakeholders across the Region. The publication highlights PAHO’s achievements, current contributions, remaining challenges, and future opportunities, emphasizing that investing in PAHO means investing in healthier populations, stronger health systems, and more resilient societies throughout the Americas.&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.identifier.govdoc&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;6da97404-25f2-4aa0-bbea-2d658fe29f0c&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;PAHO/ERP/26-0001&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.identifier.uri&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;a4834ab4-cad9-471d-9428-de744deda0e0&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;https://iris.paho.org/handle/10665.2/72318&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.language.iso&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;5ea21638-572a-47bd-a0b5-e0dd6eb66731&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;en&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.publisher&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;08d738e5-43a9-448a-9aa4-8458bfe4eb02&q;,&q;language&q;:null,&q;value&q;:&q;PAHO&q;,&q;place&q;:0,&q;authority&q;:null,&q;confidence&q;:-1}],&q;dc.rights&q;:[{&q;uuid&q;:&q;7d8ad35e-43f8-4a2b-a97b-aec1e7e56ce8&q;,&q;language&q;:&q;en&q;,&q;value&q;:&q;Attribution-NonCommercial-ShareAlike
